AATIP - "Advanced Aerospace Threat Identification Program" - name of the program initially created
by Senators Reid and Inoye in 2008.

===== PAGE 264 =====
{"alpha_words": 327, "chars": 2046, "method": "ocr", "page": 264, "variant": "plain-vision"}
AAWSAP - "Advanced Aerospace Weapon System Application Program" - name of the contract that
DIA executed and managed in accordance with the AATIP legislation.
To the questions that are being asked about Elizondo's purported role in AATIP. As Tara has said for
Garry and as 1 have said, Elizondo had no assigned responsibilities for this program. Elizondo worked
for me from 2010 to early 2012. He worked for Garry for some years prior to his resignation from the
government.
Of note and in the interest of absolute clarity:
I have seen one document that associates Elizondo with AATIP. It is a letter from Senator Reid's office,
dated 24 June 2009 that requested a security assessment. It had an attachment with a list of names of
proposed 'bigoted list of government personnel'. That list included Elizondo. That document was
marked by Sen Reid's office as containing information exempt from mandatory disclosure under FOIA,
citing exemptions 1 and 5.
That letter from Senator Reid's office pre-dates Elizondo being assigned to my office. At the time he
was assigned to me, he brought with him no responsibilities for AATIP (and I was unaware of its
existence).
That one document is the only written association of Elizondo with AATIP, it has nothing about who is
'in charge' of AATIP, and as we've said he had no responsibilities for the program while under my or
Garry Reid's oversight.
Thanks
Neill

recent FOIA release, a string of emails show Mr. Elizondo's communication with DOPSR. What I am seeking comment on, is the
most chronologically recent email in the chain. I put a screen shot below for reference.

any form to the public. However, through email, Mr. Elizondo said to broaden the request for usage to "unrestricted." Please

that if metadata was removed, the videos would be unclassified. If they did, unrestricted access would be granted by DOPSR. So,
it relied on the Navy.

Good afternoon, all. Your questions below pertain to the issues I raised in
earlier emails regarding the distinctions we must make between past programs
and the normal, day-to-day coordination/facilitation work of the DoD and, in
particular, the OUSDI. Mr. Elizondo did play a role in facilitating
connections and introductions to other DoD elements that were working
CI/safety issues similar to those encountered by Navy. There were no
funding lines associated with this work or significant resoorces expended,
and nothing out of the ordinary regarding bosiness practice. The "work" was
similar to the daily travails of all of us in the Pentagon-- office and
small group meetings, advice, and correspondences largely related to the
same. In this regard, Mr. Elizondo was very useful. Though a colorful
personality to be sore, I think Neill would agree that Mr. Elizondo
maintained a solid reputation up until he made a sudden, shocking departure
in October 2017.
As always, I am happy to discuss details in person or on another network.
As for your particular questions below--
1) 1am aware of office discussions that occurred at the request of OSD
leadership. I escorted the pilots myself.
2) Joe Gradisher covered this below, and
is copied on this message. (0G
is an Office of Naval Intelligence Defense Intelligence Senior Leader
(DISE).
3) I think you characterize the confusion accurately. The differences of
perspective opinion eas arise stame mem rim termiris ge regarding
"The DoD is always concerned about maintaining positive identification of
all aircraft in our operating environments as well as identifying any
foreign capability that may be a threat to the homeland. DoD will continue
to investigate, through normal procedures, reports of unidentified aircraft
encountered by US military aviators in order to ensure defense of the
homeland and protection against strategic surprise by oor nation's
adversaries."
Joe Gradisher can provide the official Navy TPs on this, but they are very
similar.

Subject: Elizondo & Transter of AATIP
Gentlemen,
Both Luis Elizondo himself and former DASD Chris Mellon have contacted our
office because they believe the statements that we've put out regarding
AATIP and particularly Elizondo's role in it, are false (and Elizondo is
threatening to sue). Per the information we've received from DIA and
OUSD(I), Elizondo was briefly supporting the DIA office that managed the
AATIP program, but after early 2010, while in OUSD(I), he had no
responsibilities for AATIP. Elizondo claims he continued working on
AATIP/UAPs until he resigned in 2017. He claims that (b)(6)
knew of the program/his involvement, and that then-SECDEF Mattis and/or his
front office was briefed on the program, and that two Navy pilots briefed
the SECDEF/his front office on "what they saw."
First, are you aware of any briefings on UAPs/AATIP to then-SECDEF Mattis'
front office in general, and in particular, do have any
knowledge/reports/documentation that two Navy pilots briefed the SECDEF
front office on any UAP incidents in the 2016-2017 time frame?
Second, do you know who (b)(6)
is? Does he still work for Navy
(perhaps somewhere in the SECNA V/secretariat side, rather than the military
side of Navy)? Did he previously/does he still have anything to do with
UAPs?
Third. There is confusion -- probably as a result of semantics/terminology
-- over whether the program "ended" or was "transitioned." By the best
information we've received, the formal AATIP program last received funding
in the FY2010 NDAA, and those funds ran out, with the completion of the
final 12 of 38 technical studies, in 2012. From the overall DoD
perspective, that was the end of AATIP as a program. However, I'm beginning
to think what happened is that some office in either OSD and/or Navy (e.g..
N2N6) continued to track/monitor UAP incidents -- but not as an official
program, just as a mission/function/duty of an office, with no funding line,
and certainly not as "AATIP." I think that is what Elizondo is referring to
when he claims that he continued to work with/in AATIP until 2017. Can you
shed any light on that?
Regards,
(b)(6)

{"alpha_words": 380, "chars": 2371, "method": "ocr", "page": 314, "variant": "plain-vision"}
https://www.vice.com/en/article/5dpm45/this-guy-says-he-was-the-source-of-the-
pentagons-ufo-videos
Ex Intel Official Says He Was the
Source of the Pentagon's UFO Videos
In the recently released UFO documentary The Phenomenon, Chris
Mellon, the former Deputy Assistant Secretary of Defense for
Intelligence, stated that he was the source who provided the New York
Times with the three infamous UFO videos it published in 2017.
Mellon, who is currently a member of Tom DeLonge's To the Stars
Academy, told filmmaker James Fox in an on-camera interview that he
met with an unnamed individual in the parking lot of the Pentagon and
was handed a package containing the three videos that formed the basis
of the most important UFO article in many years.
"I received the videos, the now famous videos in the Pentagon parking
lot from a Defense Department official. I still have the packaging,"
Mellon said. "This is a case where somebody bent the rules a little bit,
and they did so for the larger good and we're absolutely all better off
because of it."
Motherboard has been unable to independently verify that Mellon was
the source of the videos, but his story tracks with everything we know
about them. We know that To the Stars Academy ultimately published
the videos, and Mellon was one of the earliest members of that group.
One of the New York Times journalists who worked on that story, Leslie
Kean, also appears in the documentary. In that story, the Times unveiled
a secretive Pentagon UFO program called the Advanced Aerospace
Threat Identification Program, or AATIP, and released videos shot by
Navy pilots who intercepted a strange object off the coast of San Diego
on November 14th, 2004. The pilots managed to shoot video of the
object with their F-18's gun camera. Two other videos recorded on
January 21st, 2015, were released showing another anomalous acrial
vehicle rotating while in flight and another object quickly flying over the
water below.
Months earlier, in late August of 2017, the former head of AATIP, Luis
Elizondo, worked with the Defense Office of Prepublication and
Security Review to have the three videos declassified. On October 4th,
Kean met with Elizondo as well as other individuals where she was told
about the secret UFO program. Elizondo told Motherboard that Chris
Mellon was in the room as well, and showed Kean videos on a laptop.

===== PAGE 315 =====
{"alpha_words": 210, "chars": 1284, "method": "ocr", "page": 315, "variant": "plain-vision"}
Elizondo believes that the videos Kean was shown were the three UAP
videos in question, but could not confirm it outright because he was not
looking at the computer during that time.
Earlier that same day, Elizondo resigned from his position at the
Pentagon. Only days later, Elizondo along with Mellon would appear on
stage with forner Blink 182 punk rocker Tom DeLonge and announce a
new UFO research organization named "To The Stars Academy of Arts
and Science."
As a result, UFOs have become a hot topic. Publicly, the Department of
Detence established a new UAP Task Force on August 4th, 2020 to
continue investigating UFO reports made by military personnel.
However, previous statements by the Pentagon contradict this and seem
to indicate that the Office of Naval Intelligence along with the Office of
the Secretary of Defense has had such a task force well before August of
2020.
Motherboard reached out to Kean and she stated that due to policies
concerning source identity at the New York Times, she could not
comment. Mellon was also unable to comment at this time and declined
an interview. A spokesperson for the New York Times told Motherboard
"the Department of Defense was the on the record source of the videos
in our coverage. We don't plan to comment beyond that."

personnel who are preparing to deploy outside the United States, personnel in strategic or
national security roles, as well as key frontline workers like teachers, support staff, child
and youth services.

their vaccines. And this is truly remarkable, you know, for a vaccine to be this safe is -- is
quite heartening.

* FEMA Request for Vaccination Support: Released statement by Pentagon Press Secretary
John Kirby acknowledging receipt of request from FEMA for vaccination support: "The
Department of Defense has received a request from FEMA for assistance in administering
COVID-19 vaccine at various locations across the country. The Department is evaluating the
request, and what kinds of support it can provide. Given the significance of the request, it will

===== PAGE 355 =====
{"alpha_words": 442, "chars": 2906, "method": "ocr", "page": 355, "variant": "plain-vision"}
be reviewed urgently but carefully to determine what DoD assets can safely be made available
to support the effort. As Secretary Austin has said, DoD is committed to do as much as it
possibly can to assist the whole-of-government effort against COVID-19."
* Allegations of Violation of Libya Arms Embargo. UK Times story on UN Libya Panel of
Experts' alleging U.S. violation of Libya arms embargo resulted in additional queries from
Barbara Starr, CNN; Helene Cooper, NYT; Umar Farooq, Middle East Eye; Mikhail Turgiev, RIA
Novosti; and Dmitry Kirsanov, TASS, asking for a Pentagon comment and specifically if the U.S.
had captured a Russian missile system trom Russia in Libya. Additional questions included
what kind of missile system was this; where and when exactly was it captured; where was it
taken; how did the US come up with the plan to capture it; and have U.S. forces learned
anything from the missile system. Provided an NSC-cleared on-the-record comment that "We
are aware of the UK Times story. The Department of State can provide you more context with
regards to the United States policy towards Libya with specifics. The United States opposes all
foreign military intervention in Libya. We strongly support the October 23 Libyan ceasefire
agreement, including the immediate withdrawal of foreign military forces and mercenaries.
This includes the need for the departure of Russian proxy forces. To that end, the United
States has been a leader on promoting adherence to the arms embargo. We remain fully
committed to ensuring advanced weapons systems neither enter Libya nor are proliferated
from Libya into the hands of dangerous actors, and further stoking violent conflict in violation
of the word and spirit of the arms embargo. The United States prioritizes finding an inclusive,
negotiated, political solution to the Libyan conflict."
* Halting Onboarding Actions for DoD Board Members: Lara Seligman, Politico, and Nancy
Youssef, Wall Street Journal, asked about a recent internal message halting onboarding
actions for newly appointed DoD advisory board members. Provided the following, attributed
to Mr. Kirby: ""The Secretary, as you would expect, is reviewing current policies in place
across the Department to determine if any changes are necessary, to include the advisory
boards. No final decisions have been made with respect to board membership. But we will
make the information available should that change."

QUERIES OF NOTE
* 2020 Recruitment Figures. Andrew Jeong, WJ, asked a series of questions on whether the
2020 pandemic and its adverse effects on the job market has led to more applicants to the
Armed Forces, compared to 2019, and previous recent years: was there an increase; how
much did application numbers jump during the 2007-09 financial crisis; how many applications
did the four branches and the Coast Guard receive in 2005, 2006, 2007, 2008, and 2009; and
would there be a rough percentage based statistic available. Coordinating response with
Manpower and Reserve Affairs Office.
* UAPTF Budget. Duncan Phenix, Mystery Wire, 8 News Now (Las Vegas), asked whether a
statement on Twitter attributed to a DoD spokesperson was accurate, and asked about the
budget for the UAPTF. Confirmed the accuracy of the statement. Coordinating with Navy and
OUSD(I&5) on the budget questions.
* UAP Report to Congress. Douglas Johnson, Scientific Coalition for UAP Studies/TheDebrief,
asked for information regarding the role of the Department of Defense in responding to the
congressional directive, with regard to process, timing, or any other aspect. Responded that
we had nothing further beyond what ODNI had provided him (which was simply that ODNI is
aware of the requirement and will respond accordingly).
* Tenant Bill of Rights Status. Haley Britzky, Task and Purpose, asked about the status of the
privatized housing Tenant Bill of Rights, where four - maintenance history, dispute resolution,
rent withholding, and the right to common documents - had not been implemented yet and
were still being worked on. Coordinated with DASD(Housing) that the Department has been
working with the MHPI companies to gain their voluntary agreement to retroactively apply the
remaining requirements to existing MHPI projects. Based on those ongoing discussions, we
expect the remaining four tenant rights to be available at the majority of vast installations with
MHPI housing by June 2021.
* Service Member Financial Benefits. Brenden Middleton, The Elks Magazine, requested
information on financial benefits for service members. He wanted to know the number of

===== PAGE 358 =====
{"alpha_words": 485, "chars": 3251, "method": "ocr", "page": 358, "variant": "plain-vision"}
service members who take advantage of financial benefits and what kind of benefits are
available to spouses. Coordinating with MPP (M&RA): There are several dedicated sites that
list military pay and benefits such as Military OneSource, the Department of Defense Office of
Financial Readiness (FINRED), the Department of Defense Military Spouse Employment
Partnership (MSEP), etc. It is important to the DoD to sustain the All-Volunteer Force and
support Service members and their families. DoD utilizes its full array of pay and benefits in
order to recruit, retain, develop, motivate and assign Service members to locations around the
world and to support them and their families. The military has a pay and benefits package
that compares very favorably to the private sector in recognition of the dedication and
sacrifices our military members make in service to the nation. Amid the pandemic, the Office
of the Under Secretary of Defense for Personnel and Readiness issued military personnel
guidance addressing special policies and procedures implemented to ensure continuing
financial security for our members and their families in response to COVID-19.
* GAO Report on BAH. Rose Thayer, Stars and Stripes, requested a comment on the recent
report on BAH from the GAO. Response coordinated with M&RA and provided to the
reporter: "We appreciate GAO's thorough review of the BAH program and are gratified they
found the program to be sound and well run. We welcome their recommendations for
improvements to ensure this program better and more accurately reflects the housing costs
faced by our Service members and their families. The Military Compensation Policy staff
continuously reviews the BAH program and identifies areas for potential process
improvement. The GAO report's final recommendations are largely consistent with
improvements Military Compensation also identified for implementation in coming years. We
look forward to finding the best implementation solutions for these recommendations."
* EO on Transgender Service Members. Chris Johnson, Washington Blade, asked if discharges
under DTM-19-004 now prohibited and will be prohibited as long as the EO is in place;
whether the Department think it must implement a policy allowing individuals who obtained
treatment for gender dysphoria to accede or enlist; and whether government funds will be
used to pay for transition-related care, as well as the number of discharges and blocked
accessions under DTM-19-004. Response coordinated with M&RA and provided to the
reporter: "While the Department appreciates your interest and questions, it would be
premature to comment on prospective policy at this time. We refer you to the Executive
Order, the White House Fact Sheet, and Secretary Austin's statement for now and commit to
providing updated information as it becomes available. As you will note, the Secretary's
statement calls for 60 days to "develop the appropriate policies and procedures to implement
these changes.""
* Letter to Evolution Metals Corporation. Jacob Holzman, S&P Global Market Intelligence, and
James Munson, Bloomberg Environment, asked whether a letter allegedly signed by
representatives from DOE, DOC, and DOD and sent to Evolution Metals Corporation is

===== PAGE 359 =====
{"alpha_words": 437, "chars": 2986, "method": "ocr", "page": 359, "variant": "plain-vision"}
authentic, as well as whether the letter received an ethics review by the Department before
the DoD representative signed. Coordinating response with SOCO (OGC).
* Vaccinations for Guantanamo Detainees. Carol Rosenberg, New York Times, asked whether
or not the vaccine that will be given to Guantanamo detainees will be Moderna; who signed
the approval memo; will the defendants in the case going to arraignment on Feb. 22 go first;
and whether she can have a copy of the material that the detainees will be given explaining to
them that it is voluntary (Arabic and Bahasa Indonesian). Working response with SOUTHCOM,
Detainee Policy Office and the COVID Task Force. Her initial article is at this link:
https://www.nytimes.com/live/2021/01/28/world/covid-19-coronavirus/the-pentagon-says-
prisoners-at-guantanamo-bay-will-be-offered-vaccination. Kaylee Greenlee, The Daily Caller

offered COVID-19 vaccines. She wants to know how many vaccines will be made available,
which vaccines and whether the troops stationed there receive vaccinations before the
prisoners. Working the response with SOUTHCOM, CVTF and Detainee Policy Office.
* Iraq Announcement of Killing ISIS Deputy Commander. Zachary Halaschak, Washington
Examiner, and Tor Bowman, NPR, asked for confirmation/comment about Iraqi Prime
Minister Al-Khadimi's claim that Iraqi Security Forces eliminated ISIS deputy commander Abu
Yaser al-Issawi. Provided on-the-record comment that, "Today, Iraqi Prime Minister Mustafa
Al-Kadhimi announced that Iraqi Secuity Forces killed Abu Yaser al-Issawi, supervisor of ISIS
operations in Iraq. The U.S. has NOT confirmed al-Issawi's death. If confirmed, this is a
tremendous victory for our partners and for the Iraqi people."
* Taiwan Relationship. Jessica Ni, TVBS News of Taiwan, asked for comment on the PRC
comment regarding Taiwan's independence. Referred to Mr. Kirby's comments at the presser.
* ASD(IPSA) Nominee. Sangmin Lee, Radio Free Asia, asked who was the nominee for IPSA.
Replied that David Helvey is still Acting ASD(IPSA).
* Korean Exercises and OPCON. Nari Baek, Yonhap News agency, asked for a comment on the
ROK PM discussing OPCON transfer and exercises. Referred to Mr. Kirby's comments at the
presser for exercises, and after coordination with OSD (Policy), provided previously cleared
lines on OPCON: "Wartime Operational Control will transition when the mutually-agreed upon
conditions are fully met. A conditions-based wartime OPCON transition is not only what the
United States and Republic of Korea mutually agreed to, it is also necessary to ensure the
security of our military forces, peoples, and the region. A commitment to a specific timeframe
would put our forces and people at risk. Similarly, ensuring the security of military forces,
peoples, and the region requires is more complicated than merely swapping the leadership of
the Combined Forces Command."

{"alpha_words": 397, "chars": 2746, "method": "ocr", "page": 360, "variant": "plain-vision"}
* DPAA/PLA Dialogue. Jennifer White, NHK, and Stacy Hsu, Taiwan's Central News Agency,
asked for a comment on the story that said the DoD talked with the PLA this week.
Coordinated with OSD (Policy) and replied with "From January 25-26, 2021, the Defense
POW/MIA Accounting Agency (DPAA) researchers conducted a first-ever Virtual Archival
Exchange with their PRC counterparts at the People's Liberation Army (PLA) Archives
Department. The two-day event was a rare opportunity to seek new information, discuss
previous requests, and raise possible field investigations in the coming year with the PLA
Archives Department. There were 15 U.S. POW/MIA cases specifically discussed covering
World War (WWII), the Korean War, Cold War, and the Vietnam War. DPAA will continue to
engage with the PLA regarding these cases and the U.S. looks forward to safely executing
activities in China once COVID -19 safety protocols and operational planning requirements are
in place."

imply or constitute DoD endorsement."
*JEDI. At 4 p.m. today, the DoD CIO sent an Information Paper on the Upcoming JEDI Cloud

===== PAGE 361 =====
{"alpha_words": 214, "chars": 1450, "method": "ocr", "page": 361, "variant": "plain-vision"}
Litigation Milestone to Congress, explaining that the Court of Federal Claims (COFC) will make
a significant ruling within the coming weeks related to the Joint Enterprise Defense
Infrastructure (JEDI) Cloud contract post-award protest. The ruling is on the Government's
Motion to Dismiss, in Part, Count Four of Amazon Web Services (AWS)'s protest complaint.
Count Four relates to AWS's allegation of improper influence at the highest levels of
government that, allegedly, unfairly affected the outcome of the JEDI Cloud competition. The
parties are now waiting for the Court's ruling. Atter Congressional Notification was complete,
the information paper was sent to DoD and interested trade media.
* News Media Travel to Guantanamo. Carol Rosenberg, NYT, and John Ryan, LawDragon,

actions for newly appointed DoD advisory board members. Provided the following, attributed
to Mr. Kirby: ""The Secretary, as you would expect, is reviewing current policies in place
across the Department to determine if any changes are necessary, to include the advisory
boards. No final decisions have been made with respect to board membership. But we will
make the information available should that change."
BRIEFINGS
Pentagon Press Secretary John Kirby, COVID Senior Advisor Max Rose, and Brig. Gen. Paul
Friedrichs, Joint Staff Surgeon, briefed the press on COVID-19 efforts, vaccinations, and myriad
other topics, including Afghanistan, extremism in the ranks, Taiwan, Korean Peninsula, global
force posture, FY22 budget, SECDEF phone calls with counterparts, Israel move to CENTCOM,
and NG deployment to DC. A transcript will be posted to defense.gov when available.

QUERIES OF NOTE
* 2020 Recruitment Figures. Andrew Jeong, WSJ, asked a series of questions on whether the
2020 pandemic and its adverse effects on the job market has led to more applicants to the
Armed Forces, compared to 2019, and previous recent years: was there an increase; how
much did application numbers jump during the 2007-09 financial crisis; how many applications
did the four branches and the Coast Guard receive in 2005, 2006, 2007, 2008, and 2009; and
would there be a rough percentage based statistic available, Coordinating response with
Manpower and Reserve Affairs Office.
* UAPTF Budget. Duncan Phenix, Mystery Wire, 8 News Now (Las Vegas), asked whether a
statement on Twitter attributed to a DoD spokesperson was accurate, and asked about the
budget for the UAPTF. Confirmed the accuracy of the statement. Coordinating with Navy and
OUSD(|&5) on the budget questions.
* UAP Report to Congress. Douglas Johnson, Scientific Coalition for UAP Studies/TheDebriet,
asked for information regarding the role of the Department of Defense in responding to the
congressional directive, with regard to process, timing, or any other aspect. Responded that
we had nothing further beyond what ODNI had provided him (which was simply that ODNI is
aware of the requirement and will respond accordingly).
* Tenant Bill of Rights Status. Haley Britzky, Task and Purpose, asked about the status of the
privatized housing Tenant Bill of Rights, where four - maintenance history, dispute resolution,
rent withholding, and the right to common documents - had not been implemented yet and
were still being worked on. Coordinated with DASD(Housing) that the Department has been
working with the MHPI companies to gain their voluntary agreement to retroactively apply the
remaining requirements to existing MHPI projects. Based on those ongoing discussions, we
expect the remaining four tenant rights to be available at the majority of vast installations with
MHPI housing by June 2021.
* Service Member Financial Benefits. Brenden Middleton, The Elks Magazine, requested
information on financial benefits for service members. He wanted to know the number of
service members who take advantage of financial benefits and what kind of benefits are
available to spouses. Coordinating with MPP (M&RA): There are several dedicated sites that
list military pay and benefits such as Military OneSource, the Department of Defense Office of
Financial Readiness (FINRED), the Department of Defense Military Spouse Employment
Partnership (MSEP), etc. It is important to the DoD to sustain the All-Volunteer Force and
support Service members and their families. DoD utilizes its full array of pay and benefits in
order to recruit, retain, develop, motivate and assign Service members to locations around the

===== PAGE 368 =====
{"alpha_words": 472, "chars": 3142, "method": "ocr", "page": 368, "variant": "plain-vision"}
world and to support them and their families. The military has a pay and benefits package
that compares very favorably to the private sector in recognition of the dedication and
sacrifices our military members make in service to the nation. Amid the pandemic, the Office
of the Under Secretary of Defense for Personnel and Readiness issued military personnel
guidance addressing special policies and procedures implemented to ensure continuing
financial security for our members and their families in response to COVID-19.
* GAO Report on BAH. Rose Thayer, Stars and Stripes, requested a comment on the recent
report on BAH from the GAO. Response coordinated with M&RA and provided to the
reporter: "We appreciate GAO's thorough review of the BAH program and are gratified they
found the program to be sound and well run. We welcome their recommendations for
improvements to ensure this program better and more accurately reflects the housing costs
faced by our Service members and their families. The Military Compensation Policy staff
continuously reviews the BAH program and identifies areas for potential process
improvement. The GAO report's final recommendations are largely consistent with
improvements Military Compensation also identified for implementation in coming years. We
look forward to finding the best implementation solutions for these recommendations."
* EO on Transgender Service Members. Chris Johnson, Washington Blade, asked if discharges
under DTM-19-004 now prohibited and will be prohibited as long as the EO is in place;
whether the Department think it must implement a policy allowing individuals who obtained
treatment for gender dysphoria to accede or enlist; and whether government funds will be
used to pay for transition-related care, as well as the number of discharges and blocked
accessions under DTM-19-004. Response coordinated with M&RA and provided to the
reporter: "While the Department appreciates your interest and questions, it would be
premature to comment on prospective policy at this time. We refer you to the Executive
Order, the White House Fact Sheet, and Secretary Austin's statement for now and commit to
providing updated information as it becomes available. As you will note, the Secretary's
statement calls for 60 days to "develop the appropriate policies and procedures to implement
these changes.""
* Letter to Evolution Metals Corporation. Jacob Holzman, 5&P Global Market Intelligence, and
James Munson, Bloomberg Environment, asked whether a letter allegedly signed by
representatives from DOE, DOC, and DOD and sent to Evolution Metals Corporation is
authentic, as well as whether the letter received an ethics review by the Department before
the DoD representative signed. Coordinating response with SOCO (OGC).
* Vaccinations for Guantanamo Detainees. Carol Rosenberg, New York Times, asked whether
or not the vaccine that will be given to Guantanamo detainees will be Moderna; who signed
the approval memo; will the defendants in the case going to arraignment on Feb. 22 go first;
and whether she can have a copy of the material that the detainees will be given explaining to

===== PAGE 369 =====
{"alpha_words": 441, "chars": 3057, "method": "ocr", "page": 369, "variant": "plain-vision"}
them that it is voluntary (Arabic and Bahasa Indonesian). Working response with SOUTHCOM,
Detainee Policy Office and the COVID Task Force. Her initial article is at this link:
https://www.nytimes.com/live/2021/01/28/world/covid-19-coronavirus/the-pentagon-says-
prisoners-at-guantanamo-bay-will-be-offered-vaccination. Kaylee Greenlee, The Daily Caller
News Foundation, also asked whether the Pentagon said detainees at Guantánamo Bay will be
offered COVID-19 vaccines. She wants to know how many vaccines will be made available,
which vaccines and whether the troops stationed there receive vaccinations before the
prisoners. Working the response with SOUTHCOM, CVTF and Detainee Policy Office.
* Iraq Announcement of Killing ISIS Deputy Commander. Zachary Halaschak, Washington
Examiner, and Tom Bowman, NPR, asked for confirmation/comment about Iraqi Prime
Minister Al-Khadimi's claim that Iraqi Security Forces eliminated ISIS deputy commander Abu
Yaser al-Issawi. Provided on-the-record comment that, "Today, Iraqi Prime Minister Mustafa
Al-Kadhimi announced that Iraqi Secuity Forces killed Abu Yaser al-Issawi, supervisor of ISIS
operations in Iraq. The U.S. has NOT confirmed al-Issawi's death. If confirmed, this is a
tremendous victory for our partners and for the Iraqi people."
* Taiwan Relationship. Jessica Ni, TVBS News of Taiwan, asked for comment on the PRC
comment regarding Taiwan's independence. Referred to Mr. Kirby's comments at the presser.
* ASD(IPSA) Nominee. Sangmin Lee, Radio Free Asia, asked who was the nominee for IPSA.
Replied that David Helvey is still Acting ASD(IPSA).
* Korean Exercises and OPCON. Nari Baek, Yonhap News agency, asked for a comment on the
ROK PM discussing OPCON transfer and exercises. Referred to Mr. Kirby's comments at the
presser for exercises, and after coordination with OSD(Policy), provided previously cleared
lines on OPCON: "Wartime Operational Control will transition when the mutually-agreed upon
conditions are fully met. A conditions-based wartime OPCON transition is not only what the
United States and Republic of Korea mutually agreed to, it is also necessary to ensure the
security of our military forces, peoples, and the region. A commitment to a specific timeframe
would put our forces and people at risk. Similarly, ensuring the security of military forces,
peoples, and the region requires is more complicated than merely swapping the leadership of
the Combined Forces Command."
* DPAA/PLA Dialogue. Jennifer White, NHK, and Stacy Hsu, Taiwan's Central News Agency,
asked for a comment on the story that said the DoD talked with the PLA this week.
Coordinated with OSD(Policy) and replied with "From January 25-26, 2021, the Defense
POW/MIA Accounting Agency (DPAA) researchers conducted a first-ever Virtual Archival
Exchange with their PRC counterparts at the People's Liberation Army (PLA) Archives
Department. The two-day event was a rare opportunity to seek new information, discuss
previous requests, and raise possible field investigations in the coming year with the PLA

===== PAGE 370 =====
{"alpha_words": 401, "chars": 2779, "method": "ocr", "page": 370, "variant": "plain-vision"}
Archives Department. There were 15 U.S. POW/MIA cases specifically discussed covering
World War (WWII), the Korean War, Cold War, and the Vietnam War. DPAA will continue to
engage with the PLA regarding these cases and the U.S. looks forward to safely executing
activities in China once COVID -19 safety protocols and operational planning requirements are
in place."
ADDITIONAL ITEMS OF INTEREST
* Hypersonics. Closed out additional support to a recent interview with Sydney Freedberg,
Breaking Defense and OUSD(R&E) Principal Director for Hypersonics, Mr. Mike White, focusing
on conventional, theater hypersonic weapons, rather than intercontinental. Breaking
Defense interviewed Mr. White in regards to a recent paper released by the Union of Concern
Scientists on DOD Hypersonic programs, which was also written about in the NYT. Story
expected to post today or tomorrow.
* SECDEF Interview Request. Sean Kim, Seoul Broadcasting System, would like to interview
Secretary Austin about military issues of Asia Pacific, as well as the Korean peninsula. Request
placed in the SECDEF Interview Requests folder for review and processing.
* Cyber Policy. Eilen Nakashima, The Washington Post, has an off-the-record conversation
with Madeline Mortelmans, Principal Director Cyber Policy, Friday, Jan. 29 |4:30 p.m. to 4:45
p.m.) in order to get to know her and perhaps get some off-the-record information about
cyber policy, partnerships and the Defend Forward Strategy. Telephonic conversation will
take place on a public affairs conference line and last between 10-15 minutes.
* WAP Videos - Discovery Channel. Graham Evans, Discovery Channel, asked about using the
UAP videos he had seen on YouTube for the Discovery Channel show, "Strange Evidence."
Pointed him to where the videos were officially released on the NAVAIR FOIA Reading Room,
and requested that the show cite U.5. Navy as the source, as well as use our standard
disclaimer: "The appearance of U.S. Department of Defense (DoD) visual information does not
imply or constitute DoD endorsement."
*JEDI. At 4 p.m. today, the DoD CIO sent an Information Paper on the Upcoming JEDI Cloud
Litigation Milestone to Congress, explaining that the Court of Federal Claims (COFC) will make
a significant ruling within the coming weeks related to the Joint Enterprise Defense
Infrastructure (JEDI) Cloud contract post-award protest. The ruling is on the Government's
Motion to Dismiss, in Part, Count Four of Amazon Web Services (AWS)'s protest complaint.
Count Four relates to AWS's allegation of improper influence at the highest levels of
government that, allegedly, unfairly affected the outcome of the JEDI Cloud competition. The
parties are now waiting for the Court's ruling. After Congressional Notification was complete,

===== PAGE 371 =====
{"alpha_words": 122, "chars": 816, "method": "ocr", "page": 371, "variant": "plain-vision"}
the information paper was sent to DoD and interested trade media.
* News Media Travel to Guantanamo. Carol Rosenberg, NYT, and John Ryan, LawDragon,
confirmed that they wanted to travel to Guantanamo to observe the arraignment of three
men accused in the bombing of nightclubs in Bali, Indonesia, in 2002 and the bombing of a
J.W. Marriott hotel in Jakarta, Indonesia, in 2003. The arraignment is currently scheduled for
not later than Feb. 20. Additional reporters are undecided: Ben Fox, A; Amy Walters, Al
Jazeera English; and Julia Kastein, ARD German Public Radio. The Office of Military
Commissions provided invitation to media which had expressed an interest in travelling to
Guantanamo to see detainee operations. OMC gave a deadline of noon, Jan. 28, to RSVP.
Travel is currently planned for Feb. 4.

As referenced earlier (below), I've been working the answers to a set of questions from John Greenwald from The Black Vault,
concerning the З UAP videos that are out there and how they may have been released, etc.

Subject: RE: (U//FUG) [Non-DoD Source] New York Post questions re: AATIP
AATIP - "Advanced Aerospace Threat Identification Program" - name of the program initially created
by Senators Reid and Inouye in 2008.
AAWSAP - "Advanced Aerospace Weapon System Application Program" - name of the contract that
DIA executed and managed in accordance with the AATIP legislation.
To the questions that are being asked about Elizondo's purported role in AATIP. As Tara has said for
Garry and as I have said, Elizondo had no assigned responsibilities for this program. Elizondo worked
for me from 2010 to early 2012. He worked for Garry for some years prior to his resignation from the
government.
Of note and in the interest of absolute clarity:
I have seen one document that associates Elizondo with AATIP. It is a letter from Senator Reid's office,
dated 24 June 2009 that requested a security assessment. It had an attachment with a list of names of
proposed 'bigoted list of government personnel'. That list included Elizondo. Ihat document was
marked by Sen Reid's office as containing information exempt from mandatory disclosure under FOIA.
citing exemptions 1 and 5.
That letter from Senator Reid's office pre-dates Elizondo being assigned to my office. At the time he
was assigned to me, he brought with him no responsibilities for AATIP (and I was unaware of its
existence).
That one document is the only written association of Elizondo with AATIP, it has nothing about who is
"in charge' of AATIP, and as we've said he had no responsibilities for the program while under my or
Garry Reid's oversight.
Thanks
Neill

(b)(6)
Statement by the Department of Defense on the release of historical Navy videos
Monday, April 27, 2020 9:40:07 AM
Media colleagues,
The below statement is attributable to Ms. Sue Gough, Pentagon spokesperson.
The Department of Defense has authorized the release of three unclassified Navy videos, one taken
in November 2004 and the other two in January 2015, which have been circulating in the public
domain after unauthorized releases in 2007 and 2017. The U.S. Navy previously acknowledged that
these videos circulating in the public domain were indeed Navy videos. After a thorough review, the
department has determined that the authorized release of these unclassified videos does not reveal
any sensitive capabilities or systems, and does not impinge on any subsequent investigations of
military air space incursions by unidentified aerial phenomena. DOD is releasing the videos in order
to clear up any misconceptions by the public on whether or not the footage that has been circulating
was real, or whether or not there is more to the videos. The aerial phenomena observed in the
videos remain characterized as "unidentified." The released videos can be found at the Naval Air
Systems Command FOIA Reading Room: https://www.navair.navy.mil/foia/documents.
Regards,
Duty Officer
Defense Public Affairs Operations
